I keep feeling like anything that goes wrong is my fault, and I end up thinking I’m just a bad employee and even a bad homeowner… like I somehow deserve to lose the things I worked for.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been continuous since they started, without any periods where they completely went away?\n\nPatient: Yes, it’s been pretty continuous. It started about two years and three months ago, after I bought my townhouse and the pressure of keeping up with it started building. I don’t think it’s ever fully gone away since then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s been about two years and three months now. It was after I bought my townhouse. At first I thought it was a good step, but then the pressure of keeping up with it started building, and that’s around when all of this really started. It’s been about two years and three months now.\n\nDoctor: Has anything or anyone in your life been making you feel worse or contributing to these feelings of guilt and low mood?\n\nPatient: It’s more like… I make it worse in my own head. When something goes wrong, I can’t stop thinking it’s my fault. I feel like I’m a bad employee, and like I’m failing at being a homeowner. It feels pretty steady most of the time, but there are days where it seems heavier than usual. I can’t always tell if that’s a real worsening or just a bad day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a clear period where you felt completely back to your normal self, without these feelings of guilt and low mood, before they started again?\n\nPatient: No, not really. The low mood and guilt have been there most of the time, maybe with short periods where I could function a little better, but it never fully went away.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for most of every day for the past two years?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been there most days, pretty much every day, for a little over two years now—about two years and a month. The depression and not really being interested in things haven’t really gone away.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’ve been feeling like everything that goes wrong is somehow my fault. I keep thinking I’m a bad employee, and even a bad homeowner, like I don’t deserve what I’ve worked for and I’m going to lose it. It’s been hard to shake that feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts that everything is your fault?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on for a little over two years now… about two years and one month. The low mood and not really feeling interested in things have been there that whole time too.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past two years where you felt significantly better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No, not really for a couple of months. Looking back, the low mood and not being interested in things have been there almost the whole time. There were maybe short stretches where I could function a little better, but it never really lifted for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in pleasure from activities you once enjoyed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I used to actually enjoy planning brunches with friends, painting in the spare room, hiking, and going to networking events. Now they just feel like chores, like things I’m pretending to care about instead of something I look forward to.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy any activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I still do some of them sometimes—like brunch plans, painting, hiking, networking stuff—but it doesn’t feel enjoyable anymore. It feels more like I’m going through the motions, like I’m pretending to care about it.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of low mood and guilt usually present for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s there most of the day, pretty much every day. I keep feeling like anything that goes wrong is my fault, and I end up thinking I’m just a bad employee and even a bad homeowner… like I somehow deserve to lose the things I worked for.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been continuous since they started, without any periods where they completely went away?\n\nPatient: Yes, it’s been pretty continuous. It started about two years and three months ago, after I bought my townhouse and the pressure of keeping up with it started building. I don’t think it’s ever fully gone away since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your low mood and feelings of guilt significantly improved, and then came back?\n\nPatient: Not really in a big way. Looking back, the low mood and losing interest in things has been there almost the whole time since it started. There have been short stretches where I could function a little better, but it never felt like it really went away and then came back.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant improvement in your mood or reduction in guilt feelings that lasted for at least two months at any point since your symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No, not for that long. Looking back, the low mood and not really enjoying things have been there almost the whole time. I’ve had brief times where I could function a little better, but it didn’t feel like the guilt or the mood really lifted for two months.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms lasted for two years or more?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been about two years and three months now. It started after I bought the townhouse, when the pressure of keeping up with it started building.\n\nDoctor: Has anything significant happened in your life around the time these feelings started, like a change in job, relationship, or living situation?\n\nPatient: Yes. It was after I bought my townhouse. At first I thought it was a good step, but then the pressure of keeping up with it started building, and that’s around when all of this really started. It’s been about two years and three months now.\n\nDoctor: Has anything or anyone in your life been making you feel worse or contributing to these feelings of guilt and low mood?\n\nPatient: It’s more like… I make it worse in my own head. When something goes wrong, I can’t stop thinking it’s my fault. I feel like I’m a bad employee, and like I’m failing at being a homeowner. Sometimes it feels like I deserve to lose what I worked for.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms seemed to worsen, even if briefly?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. It feels pretty steady most of the time, but there are days where it seems heavier than usual. I can’t always tell if that’s a real worsening or just a bad day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a clear period where you felt completely back to your normal self, without these feelings of guilt and low mood, before they started again?\n\nPatient: No, not really. The depression and not really being interested in things haven’t really gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms gotten worse over the past two years?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say they’ve clearly gotten worse. It’s more like they’ve stayed around pretty consistently. It’s been more ongoing—the depression and loss of interest have been with me for about two years and a month.
